# Artificial Anasazi (Long House Valley) Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Add Artificial Anasazi — the Long House Valley, AD 800–1350 — as a fourth model kind, written from Janssen's ODD and JASSS paper with the published replication's departures as ten named switches, its GPL-2.0 data bundled beside the MIT code, reproducing the published curve (measured over 15 seeds) and showing what the departures change — in the worker engine at every speed, links, Compare, recording, Experiments (the measured `lhv-calibration` and `lhv-quirks` sweeps) and the CLI, without changing any existing run.

**Architecture:** A new core module `crates/sugarscape-core/src/anasazi/` — `valley.rs` (the five bundled data files parsed once into zones, PDSI series, hydrology, water points and the historical curve), `config.rs` (parameters, `Quirks`, schema, presets), `household.rs` (the ODD's storage, consumption, expectation and endowment rules), `random.rs` (normal draws from IEEE-exact arithmetic, so WASM and native agree) and `world.rs` (`AnasaziWorld`: the ODD's yearly schedule, farm and home search, water, fission, statistics, frames, Inspect) — plugged into milestone 9's `ModelConfig`/`Model`/`ModelWorld` as `anasazi`, with one new trait method, `finished()`. The WASM `Sim` gains `finished()` and the valley's overlay arrays; on the page the model joins `models.ts`, the host reports `finished` and the valley's state, the engine pauses at the end year, and the schema panel, display, grid overlays, toolbar readout, charts, Inspect and Experiments learn the model through the tables milestone 9 made for that.

**Tech Stack:** Rust core (`sugarscape-core`), `wasm-bindgen` (`sugarscape-wasm`), the `sugarscape` CLI (clap), TypeScript + Vite + uPlot + Vitest. No new dependencies.

**Spec:** docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-25-anasazi-design.md, and its source extraction docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-25-anasazi-extraction.md (binding where the spec refers to it; cited below as §n, A-n, D-n). The milestone 1–9 specs stay binding where not changed, in particular 2026-09-25-other-artificial-societies-design.md (and its plan, docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-25-other-artificial-societies.md, whose conventions this plan follows).

## Global Constraints

- **Licensing (binding).** `data/anasazi/` holds `Map.txt`, `adjustedPDSI.txt`, `environment.txt`, `water.txt` and `settlements.txt` **unmodified** (their SHA-256 sums are in Task 1), the package's GPL-2.0 `LICENSE` and `CITATION.cff`, a `NOTICE` (GPL-2.0, doi:10.25937/krp4-g724, separate from the MIT code) and the derived `historical.txt`. The model code is written from the ODD and JASSS as captured in the extraction: **never read, translate or paraphrase `lhv.nlogo`'s procedures**; where the extraction says "the replication does X", the code implements that behavior from the extraction's words (Decisions 3–9 spell each one out).
- **Existing models unchanged.** Every existing `GOLDEN` and `MODEL_GOLDEN` entry in `crates/sugarscape-core/tests/golden.rs` and every legacy fixture stays green and **unedited** (`MODEL_GOLDEN` gains three entries; nothing else in golden.rs changes). `tests/legacy.rs` and `tests/invariants.rs` are not edited; `tests/book.rs` only gains tests. Every existing config, share link, session file and sweep reads and runs as before.
- **One engine path.** The anasazi plugs into the milestone 9 `Model` trait, `ModelConfig`, `ModelWorld`, the WASM `Sim`, host, engine, schema panel, chart table, Compare, sweeps and CLI — no per-model copies of that machinery.
- **Honesty.** Presets, sweep descriptions, the README and the Rules panel say this reproduces Janssen's 2009 replication (itself an approximation of Axtell et al. 2002, A-20), which behaviors come from the replication rather than the written description, and how close each preset gets to the historical record as measured (Decision 21).
- **Deterministic and portable.** A world is a function of (config, seed). Sample `u32` ranges, never `usize` (`gen_range(0..n as u32)`); iterate `BTreeMap`s and vectors, never a `HashMap`; never call the platform's `f64::ln`/`exp`/`sin` in a rule (Decision 4) — golden fingerprints must come out bit-identical from `wasm-pack test`.
- **Performance.** A run of 550 years takes ~0.1 s (`lhv-published`) to ~0.4 s (`lhv-published-defaults`, ~1 050 households) natively in release; no single year may take more than a few milliseconds (Decision 13 records the measurement and the complexity).
- **Copy (verbatim):** model label **Artificial Anasazi** (presets-menu optgroup); preset ids `lhv-published`, `lhv-published-defaults`, `lhv-documented`; Compare entry **Replication vs documented — Anasazi (Compare)** (id `lhv-published-vs-documented`); color modes **Occupation**, **Zones**, **Yield** (the menu labeled **Valley**); overlays **Water sources**, **Settlements**, **Farm–home links**; schema groups **Households**, **Harvest**, **Valley**, **Replication quirks**; chart titles **Households vs historical**, **Carrying capacity**, **Fit**, **Mean stored corn**, **Births, moves and departures**; readout `AD 1142 · 213 households`; built-in sweep ids `lhv-calibration`, `lhv-quirks`; series `households`, `historical`, `fit`, `capacity`, `mean_corn`, `births`, `moves`, `departures`, `year`, in that order.
- **Names are binding across tasks** (each task's Interfaces block repeats the ones it uses): core `anasazi::{AnasaziConfig, CornRange, Quirks, Household, AnasaziWorld, AnasaziSnapshot, AnasaziInspection, AnasaziMode, SERIES, schema, presets}`, `anasazi::valley::{valley, Valley, Zone, YieldColumn, PdsiSeries, WaterPoint, pdsi_class, column_yield, WIDTH, HEIGHT, CELLS, FIRST_YEAR, LAST_YEAR}`, `anasazi::household::{store_harvest, consume, expected_harvest, split_endowment}`, `anasazi::random::{ln, normal}`, `anasazi::world::{distance2, zone_color, MILE2, FARM, HOME, CellView, HouseholdView}`, `Quirks::{ALL, NONE, NAMES}`, `AnasaziConfig::{table_2, calibrated, soil_sd, validate, changes}`, `AnasaziWorld::{new, step, run, year, households, is_finished, inspect, water_xy, settlements_xy, links_xy}`, `Model::finished`, `ModelKind::Anasazi`, `ModelConfig::Anasazi`, `ModelWorld::{Anasazi, anasazi}`, `Param::{help, with_help}`; WASM `Sim::{finished, anasazi_water, anasazi_settlements, anasazi_links}`; web `AnasaziConfig`, `Quirks`, `AnasaziStats`, `AnasaziInspection`, `ValleyCellView`, `HouseholdView` (types.ts), `isValleyView`, `calendarYear`, `ticksLeft`, `MODEL_OVERLAYS` (models.ts), `NetworkOverlay`, `NETWORKS`, `ValleyOverlay`, `VALLEY_OVERLAYS`, `ValleyState`, `Wants.valley`, `WorldSnapshot.{valley, finished}` (protocol.ts), `SimLike.{finished, anasazi_water, anasazi_settlements, anasazi_links}`, `Engine.{finished, valley, ticksLeft}`, `EngineEvent 'finished'`, `finishedNotice`, `VALLEY_OVERLAY_LABELS`, `WATER_COLOR`, `SETTLEMENT_COLOR`, `settlementRadius`, `settlements`, `readoutText`, `ReadoutWorld`, `PDSI_CLASSES`, `waterText` (valley.ts), `ChartLine.reference`, `timeAxisLabel`, `worldLines` (series-data.ts).
- Every commit message ends with a blank line and then `Claude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_01Kq7NyxbMrkNsPfAhnsVcK3`; the commit commands below pass it as a second `-m`. Stage **only** the files named in the task (`git add <paths>`, never `-A`/`.`; never `.claude/`).
- Rust tasks finish with `cargo fmt --all && cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` before committing. Code below is shown `rustfmt`-formatted; run `cargo fmt` anyway.
- Web tasks run `(cd web && npm run build && npm test)`. The build regenerates `web/src/wasm-pkg` (gitignored) with `wasm-pack` and runs `tsc --noEmit` then `vite build`; Vitest imports that package (determinism.test.ts), so always build before testing. Single test files run with `(cd web && npx vitest run src/<file>.test.ts)`.
- **TypeScript:** `strict`, `noUnusedLocals`, `noUnusedParameters`, `noFallthroughCasesInSwitch`. Unused imports fail the build — each task lists its import changes. Never pass a possibly-null child to `replaceChildren`/`append`: use `h()`, which skips `null`/`false`. Test names use the typographic apostrophe (’) inside single-quoted strings.
- **Browser checks are the controller's**, not the implementer's. Each task's "Browser (controller):" line lists the scenarios it affects; the controller runs its puppeteer pass on them (and the full list in Task 12). `?debug` exposes `window.sugarscape = { engine, compare }`.

## Why this task order

- **The core first, bottom up (Tasks 1–4):** the data and its parser (checked against the extraction's counts and the historical curve), then the documented rules that need no valley (config, quirks, a household's corn, portable normal draws), then the world itself with its plumbing into `ModelConfig`/`ModelWorld`, presets and golden entries, then the measurements that judge it (book-style tests over 15 seeds, the two sweeps) together with the CLI. Existing golden entries pin everything that must not move.
- **WASM (Task 5)** exposes the model and proves the fingerprints portable.
- **The page (Tasks 6–11):** the plumbing first — types, overlays by model, the host's `finished` and valley state, the engine's end-year pause, Compare's step cap — with the smallest possible stubs so it compiles; then one reviewable piece at a time: presets and the Rules panel (Task 7), the views (Task 8), charts (Task 9), Inspect (Task 10), Experiments and the end-to-end determinism check (Task 11).
- **Docs and the full verification last (Task 12).**

## Decisions (where the spec leaves room)

These are binding; each is repeated in the task that implements it. Every rule below was implemented in a scratch copy during planning and measured (Decision 21); the code in the tasks is that code.

1. **Module layout, data and coordinates.** `crates/sugarscape-core/src/anasazi/` with `valley.rs`, `config.rs`, `household.rs`, `random.rs`, `world.rs` and a `mod.rs` that re-exports the public names. The five data files are embedded with `include_str!("../../../../data/anasazi/…")` (native and WASM alike, ≈ 170 KB of text) and parsed once into a `&'static Valley` (`std::sync::OnceLock`); a file of the wrong size or with an unknown zone code is a parse error naming the file. **Cells are numbered in frame order**, `i = y · 80 + x`, with `x` west→east and `y` the row counted **from the north** (row 0 is the top of the picture; the extraction's northward `y` is `119 − y`). `Map.txt` token `k` is `(x, y) = (k div 120, k mod 120)` (column-major, each column north→south, §5.2). Water points: `x = 25 + trunc((E − 2392)/93.5)`, northward `45 + trunc(37.6 + (N − 7954)/93.5)` (§5.5, A-8), off-grid points dropped (the two type-4 points). Settlement positions are not read (display-only in the replication, D-5). Inspect, overlays, `locate` and the agents CSV all use frame coordinates.
2. **Ticks and years.** Tick 0 is the start year's initial state — soil quality and the initial households, no harvest — and step `k` simulates year `start_year + k`; statistics are recorded after each year's fission (§6) and at tick 0. The world is **finished** when `year == end_year` (tick 550 for 800–1350): `step` and `run` then do nothing, and the new `Model::finished()` (default `false` for every other model) says so. The replication also simulates a year-800 step (551 steps, §3.1); here AD 800 is the setup, so a run is 550 steps and 551 records. `fit` is the running sum of `(households − historical)²` over every recorded year, tick 0 included (JASSS ¶4.6's L2 over 800–1350).
3. **A year, in the ODD's order (§3.1), one random order for all of it.** Year `Y = year + 1`: (0) this year's zone yields `y` (the zone's column of JASSS Table 3 at the class of the zone's PDSI for `Y`), `BY = y · q · Ha` per cell, the cells with `BY ≥ need` (the *productive* list, also `capacity`), and which zones allow homes (hydro ≤ 0 for `Y`, A-4); shuffle the household ids once; (1–2) in that order each household draws its harvest noise, ages first if `age_before_death_check`, harvests `H0 = BY(farm) · (1 + σahv · z)` (not clamped, A-14), stores it (`S-2 ← S-1 ← S0 ← H0`, the oldest leftovers spoil), eats `need` oldest-first exactly as ODD p.4 writes it (a negative slot therefore *raises* the need left), and is removed if the need left is above 0 or `age > death_age`; (3–6) in the same order each survivor computes `E[H] = S0 + S-1 + H0` (every slot but the oldest, plus `H0`) and, if `E[H] < need`, moves (Decision 5); (7) in the same order each survivor that is still there tries fission (Decision 7) — households born this year do not; (8) water becomes `Y`'s (Decision 6); (9) every household ages by one, newborns included, unless `age_before_death_check`. RNG order: one `normal` per household in step 1 (drawn before the death check), then tie-break draws and fission draws as they happen.
4. **Soil, harvest noise and portable normals.** Soil quality is drawn once at setup for **every** cell in cell order: `q = max(0, 1 + σ · z)` (§3.3), with `σ = spatial_sd`, or `annual_sd` under `single_harvest_variance` (A-1). `z` is a standard normal from Marsaglia's polar method (`u, v = 2·U − 1` until `0 < s = u² + v² < 1`, then `u · √(−2 ln s / s)`; the second value is discarded) where `ln` is computed from IEEE-exact `+ − × ÷` only (`x = m · 2^e`, `m ∈ [√½, √2)`, `ln m = 2 atanh((m−1)/(m+1))` summed by Horner's rule over 20 terms): the platform's `f64::ln` may differ in the last bit between native and wasm32, and the harvest feeds every fingerprint. `sqrt` is IEEE-exact.
5. **Farms and homes (documented, §3.8–3.9, with the adopted rules).** *Farm:* of the productive cells that are unfarmed, have no one counted as living there (`settled == 0`), are not Empty and — unless `no_farm_water_check` (A-3) — lie within a mile of water (`d² ≤ 256`, 16 cells = 1600 m at 100 m a cell, §2.1), the one nearest the searcher's current farm (squared Euclidean distance in cells; across the edges only with `wrap_edges`, A-9); ties drawn uniformly (`gen_range(0..n as u32)`, drawn only when `n > 1`, A-10). A mover's own farm is freed before it searches. No farm → the household leaves the valley (A-18). *Home:* among cells that are unfarmed and whose zone allows homes this year (hydro ≤ 0, adopted A-4 — so never the North or Mid Valley floor), tier (i)+(ii)+(iii): within a mile of the new farm **and** with this year's zone yield `y` below the farm's (A-7, adopted); else tier (i)+(ii): within a mile; else tier (i): anywhere. In the first non-empty tier, the cell **closest to water** — the smallest squared distance to the nearest water cell — ties at random. (This is the ODD's rule read directly; the replication's two-stage "nearest water cell to the farm, then nearest cell to it" lookup is not among the spec's adopted behaviors or quirks, so it is not implemented.) Empty cells may be homes (the ODD does not exclude them; neither does the replication). No home → the household leaves (its farm is freed). *Initial households* (§4): age `gen_range(0..29)` (the replication's reading of "[0, 29]"), corn (Decision 8, `initial_corn_per_slot`), then a uniform random cell `gen_range(0..9600)` from which the farm rule picks the nearest farm (by `y · q ≥ need` with `initial_eligibility_ignores_adjustment`, A-13), then a home; one that finds neither is not added (ids stay consecutive).
6. **Water (A-5, adopted in both modes).** A cell is water in year `Y` if a type-2 point is on it, a type-3 point with `start ≤ Y ≤ end` is on it, `Y` is in an alluvium period `[420, 560) [630, 680) [980, 1120) [1180, 1230)` and the cell is General, North, Mid or Kinbiko, `Y` is in a stream period `[280, 360) [800, 930) [1300, 1450)` and the cell is Kinbiko, or it is one of the eight stream cells (data coordinates (72,114), (70,113), (69,112), (68,111), (67,110), (66,109), (65,108), (65,107)). Types 1 and 4 are never water. Setup computes the start year's water; step 8 computes the year's, so a year's moves use the previous year's water (the ODD's order; §3.1's "water lags one year"). The squared distance from every cell to the nearest water cell is recomputed only when the set changes (about 30 times a run): per column the nearest water row, then per row the nearest over columns (exact; wrapping distances with `wrap_edges`).
7. **Fission (§3.10).** Eligible: `fertility_start < age ≤ fertility_end` (A-16). With `fission_needs_free_farm` (A-12) a household is skipped — without a draw — while no productive cell is farmable. Then `gen_bool(fission_probability)`; on success the child (next id, age 0) takes the farm rule from the parent's farm and then a home; if either fails nothing happens and the parent keeps its corn. Endowment: documented — the child takes `child_endowment` of each of the parent's slots, which the parent loses (corn conserved); `fission_fresh_endowment` (A-12) — the parent still keeps `1 − fcs` of each slot, and the child's slot `k` is `fcs / (1 − fcs) ×` a fresh initial-corn draw (not conserved). `births` counts established children.
8. **The ten quirks, exactly** (`quirks` in the config; each `bool`, default `true`; `Quirks::ALL` in the published presets, `Quirks::NONE` in `lhv-documented`): `age_before_death_check` (A-2: age at step 1, before the death check; no aging at step 9) · `no_farm_water_check` (A-3: no water radius for farms) · `uplands_single_class` (A-6: Arable Uplands' PDSI is 0, so always 547 kg, instead of the Natural series) · `wrap_edges` (A-9: every distance wraps, 80 × 120 torus) · `initial_corn_per_slot` (A-11: an initial-corn draw in each of the three slots; off: one draw, in `S0`) · `fission_fresh_endowment` (A-12, Decision 7) · `fission_needs_free_farm` (A-12, Decision 7) · `initial_eligibility_ignores_adjustment` (A-13: initial farms by `y · q`) · `occupancy_leak` (A-19: every settling — initial, move, fission — counts **two** in `settled`, a mover never frees its old home, each removal frees one, so a cell once lived on stays closed to farming; off: counts are exact) · `single_harvest_variance` (A-1: soil uses the annual s.d.). The Dunes' constant 855 kg, Kinbiko on the North series, hydro, water and 93.5 m are not quirks (adopted, Decisions 1, 5, 6).
9. **Ambiguities the spec does not list, resolved.** Initial corn off-quirk: one draw of `initial_corn`, held in `S0` (ODD "corn stocks … [2000, 2400]" read as the total). Storage generalizes to `storage_years + 1` slots; `E[H]` excludes only the oldest. Carrying capacity is **this** year's count of `BY ≥ need` cells (the spec's; the replication lags it a year, §6) without water or occupancy filters. Arable Uplands read environment group 3 (Natural) for hydro, Dunes and Empty 0 — over 800–1350 group 3 is always 0, so this agrees with §5.4's "count as 0" (a test checks it). A live harvest-adjustment change recomputes this year's yields at once (no RNG).
10. **Config and Rules panel.** `AnasaziConfig` (`#[serde(default, deny_unknown_fields)]`, tagged `"model": "anasazi"`): `start_year` 800, `end_year` 1350, `initial_households` 14, `initial_corn {min 2000, max 2400}`, `need` 800, `fertility_start` 16, `fertility_end`, `death_age`, `fission_probability`, `child_endowment` 0.33, `storage_years` 2, `harvest_adjustment`, `spatial_sd`, `annual_sd`, `quirks`. `Default` is `lhv-published` (as milestone 9's defaults are their first preset); `Quirks` defaults to all on, so a partial `quirks` object keeps the others on. Validation: years within the data (382–1499, `end_year > start_year`), `initial_households ≤ 1000`, `0 ≤ corn.min ≤ corn.max`, `need > 0`, `fertility_start ≤ fertility_end`, `death_age` 1–200, `fission_probability` 0–1, `child_endowment` in `[0, 1)`, `storage_years ≤ 10`, the three harvest numbers finite in 0–10. **Live:** `harvest_adjustment` and `annual_sd`; everything else is reset-only (`changes` names each differing field, e.g. `quirks`). Schema (`Param` gains an optional `help`, serialized only when set, shown under the control): **Households** (reset) — Households at the start 0–200, Their corn (kg) range 0–10 000 step 100, Food need 100–2000, Fission when older than 0–60, Fission until age 0–60, Death age 1–100, Fission probability 0–1 step 0.005, Child’s share of the corn 0–0.95, Corn keeps (years) 0–10; **Harvest** (live) — Harvest adjustment 0–2 step 0.01, Harvest s.d. (each year) 0–1; **Valley** (reset; the spec's three groups plus this one, because soil quality is drawn once and the years shape the run) — Start year, End year, Soil quality s.d.; **Replication quirks** (reset) — one switch per quirk with its one-line explanation citing A-n.
11. **Statistics, frames, Inspect, fingerprint.** Series in order `households, historical, fit, capacity, mean_corn, births, moves, departures, year`; `population()` is the household count. Frame 80 × 120 (row 0 north); modes **Occupation** (farms `LENDER` green, homes `BOTH` yellow, other cells their zone color at 30 % over the background — the default, so households show with no overlay on), **Zones** (after the ODD's Figure 1 on a dark ground: General `#8a7a52`, North `#c0504d`, Dunes `#e8e2cf`, Mid `#9a9a9a`, Nonarable `#5c5230`, Arable `#4f7fbf`, Kinbiko `#d977b0`, Empty background), **Yield** (`lerp(BACKGROUND, SUGAR, BY / (1.5 · need))`). Inspect JSON `{ site: { x, y, zone, zone_name, pdsi, pdsi_class, zone_yield, quality, base_yield, water, water_near, habitable, farmed_by, residents }, agent: household | null }` where the household is the cell's farmer, else its lowest-id resident: `{ id, age, corn, stock, harvest, expected, farm: [x, y], home: [x, y] }`; `locate(id)` is its farm. Agents CSV `id,farm_x,farm_y,home_x,home_y,age,corn,harvest,expected`. Overlays: `water_xy()` `[x, y, …]`, `settlements_xy()` `[x, y, households, …]` (actual residents, not leak counts), `links_xy()` `[fx, fy, hx, hy, …]`. Fingerprint: FNV-1a over the tick, every cell's soil-quality bits, and per household (id order) id, `farm << 32 | home`, age and each corn slot's bits.
12. **Presets** (`anasazi::presets()`, after Ring World's in the catalog): `lhv-published` = JASSS Table 4 (death age 38, fertility end 34, fission 0.155, harvest adjustment **0.56**, both s.d.s 0.4) with every quirk — Table 4's 0.56, not the NetLogo slider's 0.54 (A-15), which the description says; `lhv-published-defaults` = ODD Table 2 with every quirk; `lhv-documented` = Table 4's values, no quirks. Descriptions carry the measurements (Decision 21).
13. **Performance** (measured natively in release during planning). Farm search scans only the productive list (≤ ~1 100 cells), not all 9 600: O(P) per mover. Home search scans the 33 × 33 disk around the farm (≤ 1 089 cells), falling back to all cells only when the disk has no allowed cell. The water-distance field costs O(W·H·(W+H)) ≈ 1.9 M operations, recomputed only when water changes. Mean and worst year: `lhv-published` 0.17 ms / 1.4 ms, `lhv-published-defaults` (≈ 1 050 households) 0.76 ms / 2.5 ms, `lhv-documented` 0.05 ms / 1.1 ms; setup 1.3–2.6 ms. A whole run is 0.09 s / 0.41 s / 0.06 s, so the 105-run calibration sweep takes ~10 s single-threaded.
14. **WASM.** `Sim::finished()`; `anasazi_water()`, `anasazi_settlements()`, `anasazi_links()` (empty for other models). The WASM fingerprint of `lhv-published` after 200 ticks equals the native golden entry (`wasm-pack test`), which is the portability check of Decision 4.
15. **The page's plumbing.** `Overlay` = `NetworkOverlay` (the six sugarscape networks, `NETWORKS`) | `ValleyOverlay` (`'water' | 'settlements' | 'links'`, `VALLEY_OVERLAYS`); `OVERLAYS` lists all nine, `noOverlays()` all off. `MODEL_OVERLAYS` (models.ts) says which a model draws; `clampDisplay` turns off, in another model, every overlay that model does not draw (so the anasazi keeps its own), and in a sugarscape the valley's (`overlayAvailable` is false for them). `Wants.networks`/`WorldSnapshot.networks` are typed `NetworkOverlay`. `Wants.valley` → `WorldSnapshot.valley: ValleyState { water, settlements, links }` (Uint32Arrays), answered only by the anasazi; the engine asks for it with every request while its world is the anasazi (as it does the ring). `WorldSnapshot.finished: true` whenever the world is finished. The host's Max loop also ends when the world is finished (posting the world there, as at `MAX_TICKS`); a `step` on a finished world answers normally (nothing moves). `Engine.finished`, `Engine.valley`, `Engine.ticksLeft`; a step or post that arrives finished pauses the engine and fires **`'finished'`** (again for every later step, like `'full'`); `finishedNotice(config, tick)` = "This run has reached its end year (AD 1350) — Reset to run it again". Compare: `Lockstep` never asks either world for more than `min(ticksLeft)` ticks and pauses at 0 (so worlds with different end years stay in step), and pauses on `'finished'`; main shows A's notice; B's shows only when B's end year comes first.
16. **Views.** The Agents menu shows **Occupation / Zones / Yield** labeled **Valley**; the Landscape menu is hidden; three checkboxes (**Water sources**, **Settlements**, **Farm–home links**) show only for the anasazi. `GridView` draws them from `Engine.valley` over the frame: water as half-cell squares `#4fc3ff`, links as faint `--text` lines (through `wrappedSegments`), settlements as `#ffe04d` dots of radius `min(1.5, 0.3 + 0.2 √n)` cells with a black edge. The toolbar readout is `readoutText`: `AD 1142 · 213 households` (Compare: `AD 1142 · A 213 · B 190 households`), other models unchanged. `body[data-model='anasazi']` narrows the 80 × 120 grid to `min(100%, 54vh)` so it fits the viewport.
17. **Charts.** `MODEL_CHARTS.anasazi`: **Households vs historical** (`households` "Simulated" `--c1`; `historical` "Historical estimate" `--muted`, a **reference** line), **Carrying capacity** (`capacity`), **Fit** (`fit`), **Mean stored corn** (`mean_corn`), **Births, moves and departures**. A reference line is drawn once, from A, in Compare (`worldLines`). The anasazi's time charts are labeled **Year** and their x values are `start_year + tick` (per world).
18. **Inspect.** Rows: Cell `(x, y) · zone name`, PDSI `value · class (−1, 1)`, Yield `BY kg this year (zone y kg × soil q × adjustment)`, Water (a water source / within a mile of water / more than a mile from water), Homes (allowed / not allowed this year (hydrology)), Farmed by (link), Residents (links); then the household: Household `#id · age n`, Corn `kg (slots, newest first)`, Harvest `kg this year · expects kg next`, Farm and Home (buttons that select those cells). A tracked household that is gone: "Household #id is gone: it died or left the valley."
19. **Experiments and the CLI.** `defaultForm('anasazi')`: x `harvest_adjustment` `0.5:0.62:0.02`, final `fit`, 550 ticks, 3 seeds. Built-in sweeps (seventh and eighth): `lhv-calibration` (base `lhv-published`, x harvest adjustment 0.50–0.62 step 0.02, final `fit`, seeds 1–15, 550 ticks) and `lhv-quirks` (base `lhv-published`, x the single value 0.56, series: none off, each quirk off alone, all off — 12 lines, labeled by the Rules panel's names; final `fit`; seeds 1–15; 550 ticks). `sugarscape run` prints `finished at tick 550 (its end year)` on stderr when a run stops at its end year before the ticks asked for.
20. **Recording.** Nothing model-specific: `worldViews` records the grid canvas, which `GridView` has already drawn the valley's overlays on.
21. **Measured results and thresholds** (release, seeds 1–15, recorded 2026-09-25; `measure_long_house_valley`; historical means over the same windows in brackets):
    - `lhv-published`: first plateau (mean 1050–1130) **171.6** [156.4], dip (1140–1170) **93.6** [132.9], second plateau (1180–1265) **180.1** [171.7], 1300 **58.7** [0], 1350 **21.9** [0], no seed died out; carrying capacity (seed 1) 190 at both plateaus and 85 in the dip; mean fit **922 516** (seeds 739 610–2 134 300). JASSS Figure 10 (Table 4's values — the calibration this preset uses; the spec's Figure 4 is the same shape from Axtell's values): ABM ≈ 180 at both plateaus, ≈ 90 in the dip, falling after 1270 to 50–75 and persisting to 1350, carrying capacity ≈ 180. **It matches**: the plateau levels, the dip, the fall and the failure to empty the valley (¶4.13) all reproduce; our plateaus sit ~10 households above Figure 10's.
    - `lhv-published-defaults`: **1 045.7**, **864.0**, **1 045.4**, **481.5**, **384.9**; capacity 1 050 at the plateaus and 851 in the dip; mean fit 228 899 102. JASSS Figure 2: ≈ 1 050 households tracking capacity, a dip to ≈ 820, ≈ 400–450 by 1350. **It matches.**
    - `lhv-documented`: **41.0**, **41.8**, **79.6**, **25.5**, **8.4**; 7 of 15 seeds with no households left in 1350; mean fit **4 296 013** (4.7 × the replication's). **The written model does not reproduce the published curve**, and `lhv-quirks` shows why: turning `fission_fresh_endowment` off alone gives 4 328 619; every other quirk off alone stays within 0.86–1.34 × (age 919 196, farm water 1 034 221, uplands 966 794, wrap 858 177, initial corn 975 073, free farm 1 233 239, initial eligibility 864 268, leak 924 814, one s.d. 922 516 — identical, since both s.d.s are 0.4). Reported to the user; recorded in the preset's description and the README.
    - `lhv-calibration`: 5 384 804, 3 980 724, 1 766 489, **922 516**, 1 570 688, 2 124 817, 4 073 957 at 0.50…0.62 — best at 0.56, as JASSS ¶4.11 (0.54–0.56).
    - Thresholds (rules: levels ±20 % of the measurement rounded outward to a multiple of 10; ratios the measurement + 0.1 rounded up to a multiple of 0.05; the end level a quarter of the measurement rounded down to a multiple of 5; the fit bound 1.3 × rounded up to a multiple of 100 000): `LHV_FIRST` 130–210, `LHV_SECOND` 140–220, `LHV_DIP_AT_MOST` 0.65 (of the first plateau; measured 0.545), `LHV_FALL_AT_MOST` 0.45 (1300 against the second; 0.326), `LHV_END_AT_LEAST` 5, `LHV_FIT_AT_MOST` 1 200 000; `DEFAULTS_FIRST`/`DEFAULTS_SECOND` 830–1260, `DEFAULTS_DIP_AT_MOST` 0.95 (0.826), `DEFAULTS_FALL_AT_MOST` 0.6 (0.461), `DEFAULTS_END_AT_LEAST` 95. Calibration: best at 0.54 or 0.56 and both ends ≥ 2 × the best (measured 5.8 × and 4.4 ×). Quirks: exactly the fresh-endowment and all-off lines ≥ 2 × none; one-s.d. equal to none.
    - **Re-measuring:** `cargo test -p sugarscape-core --release --test book measure_long_house_valley -- --ignored --nocapture` (and the two sweeps with `cargo run --release -p sugarscape-cli -- sweep --builtin lhv-calibration --quiet --summary-csv /dev/stdout`). An implementation that follows this plan exactly reproduces these numbers bit for bit (the golden entries of Decision 22 pin the RNG order). **If the numbers differ, stop and report** rather than retune: the difference is a deviation from this plan. If a deliberate, reviewed change moves them, apply the rules above to the new figures and update the constants, their comments, the preset and sweep descriptions and the README together.
22. **Golden entries** (200 ticks from seed 1, `MODEL_GOLDEN`): `lhv-published` 0x3b357e6f0cc5f74a, `lhv-published-defaults` 0x7cdec8b85b1f909a, `lhv-documented` 0x33ffb6476e824b0. The same values come out of the WASM build (`wasm-pack test` checks `lhv-published`, and determinism.test.ts checks it through the page's engine). If an implementation that follows this plan exactly gives different values, stop and report rather than re-recording: the RNG order is part of the specification.

- [ ] **Step 1: Copy the five data files, the license and the citation, unmodified, and the historical curve**

The package (*Artificial Anasazi* v1.1.0, Janssen, CoMSES, doi:10.25937/krp4-g724, GPL-2.0) and the extraction's historical curve were downloaded during specification to the planning session's scratch directory:

```bash
SRC=/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-nathan-Projects-ndouglas-SugarScape/67271073-33f3-4e8f-a052-6ec42a1a6002/scratchpad/anasazi
mkdir -p data/anasazi
for f in Map adjustedPDSI environment water settlements; do cp "$SRC/aa/code/$f.txt" data/anasazi/; done
cp "$SRC/aa/LICENSE" "$SRC/aa/CITATION.cff" data/anasazi/
{ cat "$SRC/hist.txt"; echo; } > data/anasazi/historical.txt
```

If that directory is gone, download the package from https://doi.org/10.25937/krp4-g724 (the "Artificial Anasazi" 1.1.0 archive), take the same files from its `code/` directory and its root, and derive `historical.txt` from `settlements.txt` by the extraction's §5.6 rule (a data derivation, not model code):

```bash
python3 - <<'EOF'
t = [float(x) for x in open('data/anasazi/settlements.txt').read().split()]
rows = [t[i:i + 12] for i in range(0, len(t), 12)]
def n(r, y):
    start, end, med, b = int(r[3]), int(r[4]), 1950 - int(r[5]), int(r[11])
    if y < start or y >= end: return 0
    if y > med: return max(-(-b * (end - y) // (end - med)), 1)
    if med == start: return 0
    return max(-(-b * (y - start) // (med - start)), 1)
with open('data/anasazi/historical.txt', 'w') as f:
    for y in range(800, 1351): f.write(f"{y} {sum(n(r, y) for r in rows if r[6] == 1)}\n")
EOF
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the failing tests**

Create `crates/sugarscape-core/src/anasazi/mod.rs`:
```rust
//! Artificial Anasazi (milestone 10): the Long House Valley, AD 800–1350,
//! after Janssen's ODD (2013) and "Understanding Artificial Anasazi" (JASSS
//! 12(4) 13, 2009), with the published replication's departures as named
//! switches. See docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-25-anasazi-design.md and
//! its source extraction, docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-25-anasazi-extraction.md
//! (cited below as §n, A-n, D-n). The model is written from those
//! descriptions; the NetLogo code was read only for data layouts.

pub mod valley;
```

In `crates/sugarscape-core/src/lib.rs`, after `pub mod agent;` add `pub mod anasazi;`.

Create `crates/sugarscape-core/src/anasazi/valley.rs` with its module doc and the tests only (the implementation comes in Step 5):
```rust
//! The Long House Valley's data (milestone 10): zones, adjusted PDSI,
//! per-zone hydrology, water points and the archaeological household
//! estimate, parsed from the five files of *Artificial Anasazi* v1.1.0
//! (Janssen, CoMSES doi:10.25937/krp4-g724, GPL-2.0) bundled unmodified in
//! `data/anasazi/` (see `data/anasazi/NOTICE`). Layouts and rules are those
//! of docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-25-anasazi-extraction.md (§5, A-4 to
//! A-8); this module only reads the files.
//!
//! Cells are numbered in frame order: `i = y * WIDTH + x` with `x` west to
//! east and `y` the row counted from the **north** edge (the data's
//! northward coordinate is `HEIGHT - 1 - y`).

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    const HISTORICAL: &str = include_str!("../../../../data/anasazi/historical.txt");

    fn count(zone: Zone) -> usize {
        (0..CELLS).filter(|&i| valley().zone(i) == zone).count()
    }

    #[test]
    fn zones_match_the_extraction_and_jasss() {
        let v = valley();
        let counts: Vec<usize> = Zone::ALL.iter().map(|&z| count(z)).collect();
        assert_eq!(counts, [637, 328, 35, 51, 15, 3396, 145, 27, 4966]);
        // JASSS ¶4.3: 406 cells in the North and Mid Valley and Kinbiko, 637
        // in the General Valley, 50 in the Dunes.
        assert_eq!(
            count(Zone::North) + count(Zone::Mid) + count(Zone::Kinbiko),
            406
        );
        assert_eq!(count(Zone::NorthDunes) + count(Zone::MidDunes), 50);
        // Orientation (JASSS Fig 1): North Valley in the upper right, Kinbiko
        // in the upper left, the General Valley running south.
        let mean = |zone: Zone| {
            let cells: Vec<usize> = (0..CELLS).filter(|&i| v.zone(i) == zone).collect();
            let n = cells.len() as f64;
            let x = cells.iter().map(|&i| (i % 80) as f64).sum::<f64>() / n;
            let y = cells.iter().map(|&i| (i / 80) as f64).sum::<f64>() / n;
            (x, y)
        };
        let (nx, ny) = mean(Zone::North);
        let (kx, ky) = mean(Zone::Kinbiko);
        let (_, gy) = mean(Zone::General);
        assert!(
            nx > 40.0 && ny < 60.0,
            "North Valley upper right: {nx} {ny}"
        );
        assert!(kx < 40.0 && ky < 60.0, "Kinbiko upper left: {kx} {ky}");
        assert!(gy > ny, "the General Valley lies south of the North Valley");
    }

    #[test]
    fn map_codes_are_read_column_major_from_the_north() {
        // Token k is column k / 120, row k % 120 from the north.
        let map = "10 ".to_string() + &"60 ".repeat(CELLS - 2) + "0";
        let v = Valley::parse(&map, PDSI, ENVIRONMENT, WATER, SETTLEMENTS).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(v.zone(0), Zone::North, "x 0, the northern row");
        assert_eq!(v.zone(CELLS - 1), Zone::General, "x 79, the southern row");
        assert_eq!(v.zone(1), Zone::Empty, "token 1 is (0, 1), not (1, 0)");
    }

    #[test]
    fn files_of_the_wrong_size_or_content_are_refused() {
        let e = Valley::parse("0 0", PDSI, ENVIRONMENT, WATER, SETTLEMENTS).unwrap_err();
        assert!(e.starts_with("Map.txt: 2 numbers"), "{e}");
        let bad = MAP.replacen("60", "61", 1);
        let e = Valley::parse(&bad, PDSI, ENVIRONMENT, WATER, SETTLEMENTS).unwrap_err();
        assert!(e.contains("unknown zone code 61"), "{e}");
        let e = Valley::parse(MAP, "x", ENVIRONMENT, WATER, SETTLEMENTS).unwrap_err();
        assert!(e.starts_with("adjustedPDSI.txt"), "{e}");
    }

    #[test]
    fn pdsi_classes_follow_the_interval_notation() {
        let classes: Vec<usize> = [
            -5.0, -3.0, -2.9, -1.0, -0.99, 0.0, 0.99, 1.0, 2.99, 3.0, 8.0,
        ]
        .iter()
        .map(|&p| pdsi_class(p))
        .collect();
        assert_eq!(classes, [0, 0, 1, 1, 2, 2, 2, 3, 3, 4, 4]);
        assert_eq!(column_yield(YieldColumn::NorthMidKinbiko, 4), 1153.0);
        assert_eq!(column_yield(YieldColumn::General, 4), 961.0);
        assert_eq!(column_yield(YieldColumn::Dunes, 2), 855.0);
        assert_eq!(column_yield(YieldColumn::Uplands, 2), 547.0);
        assert_eq!(column_yield(YieldColumn::Uplands, 0), 411.0);
    }

    #[test]
    fn pdsi_series_are_read_by_year_and_the_dunes_have_none() {
        let v = valley();
        // Blocks 1 and 2 are categorical (§5.3, D-3).
        for year in 800..=1350 {
            for s in [PdsiSeries::North, PdsiSeries::Mid] {
                assert!([0.0, 2.0, 4.0].contains(&v.pdsi(s, year)), "{s:?} {year}");
            }
        }
        // General's classes over 800–1350 (§5.3's counts).
        let mut classes = [0; 5];
        for year in 800..=1350 {
            classes[pdsi_class(v.pdsi(PdsiSeries::General, year))] += 1;
        }
        assert_eq!(classes, [31, 77, 85, 105, 253]);
        assert_eq!(v.zone_pdsi(Zone::NorthDunes, 1000, false), 0.0);
        assert_eq!(
            v.zone_pdsi(Zone::Kinbiko, 1000, false),
            v.pdsi(PdsiSeries::North, 1000)
        );
        assert_eq!(v.zone_pdsi(Zone::Uplands, 1000, true), 0.0);
        assert_eq!(
            v.zone_pdsi(Zone::Uplands, 1000, false),
            v.pdsi(PdsiSeries::Natural, 1000)
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn around_1260_about_1050_cells_can_feed_a_household_at_full_harvest() {
        // §5.3's sanity check (JASSS ¶4.3): with Ha = 1 and every q = 1.
        let v = valley();
        let fed = (0..CELLS)
            .filter(|&i| {
                let zone = v.zone(i);
                zone.column().is_some_and(|c| {
                    column_yield(c, pdsi_class(v.zone_pdsi(zone, 1260, true))) >= 800.0
                })
            })
            .count();
        assert_eq!(fed, 406 + 637 + 50);
    }

    #[test]
    fn hydro_only_allows_residences_where_the_extraction_says() {
        let v = valley();
        for year in 800..=1350 {
            assert!(v.hydro(Zone::North, year) >= 8.0, "North {year}");
            assert!(v.hydro(Zone::Mid, year) >= 6.0, "Mid {year}");
            assert_eq!(v.hydro(Zone::Natural, year), 0.0, "Natural {year}");
            assert_eq!(v.hydro(Zone::Uplands, year), 0.0, "Uplands {year}");
            assert_eq!(v.hydro(Zone::NorthDunes, year), 0.0);
            let dry = v.hydro(Zone::General, year) <= 0.0;
            assert_eq!(dry, (877..=919).contains(&year), "General {year}");
            assert_eq!(v.hydro(Zone::Kinbiko, year) <= 0.0, dry, "Kinbiko {year}");
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn water_points_convert_onto_the_uplands_by_the_valley_floor() {
        let v = valley();
        let kinds = |k: u32| v.water_points().iter().filter(|p| p.kind == k).count();
        // Types 1 = 56, 2 = 38, 3 = 12; the two type-4 points map off the grid.
        assert_eq!((kinds(1), kinds(2), kinds(3), kinds(4)), (56, 38, 12, 0));
        let on_uplands = v
            .water_points()
            .iter()
            .filter(|p| v.zone((p.y * WIDTH + p.x) as usize) == Zone::Natural)
            .count();
        // "Nearly all" (§5.5): 95 of the 106 on-grid points.
        assert_eq!(on_uplands, 95);
    }

    #[test]
    fn water_sources_follow_the_adopted_periods() {
        let v = valley();
        let at = |year: u32| v.water_sources(year);
        let floor =
            |w: &[bool], zone: Zone| (0..CELLS).filter(|&i| v.zone(i) == zone && w[i]).count();
        // 1000 is an alluvium year: the whole valley floor is water.
        let w = at(1000);
        assert_eq!(floor(&w, Zone::General), 637);
        assert_eq!(floor(&w, Zone::North), 328);
        // 1150 is not: only points, Kinbiko outside its stream periods is dry.
        let w = at(1150);
        assert!(floor(&w, Zone::General) < 10);
        assert_eq!(floor(&w, Zone::Kinbiko), 0);
        // 850 is a stream year for Kinbiko; the periods are half-open.
        assert_eq!(floor(&at(850), Zone::Kinbiko), 27);
        assert_eq!(floor(&at(930), Zone::Kinbiko), 0);
        assert_eq!(floor(&at(1119), Zone::General), 637);
        assert!(floor(&at(1120), Zone::General) < 637);
        // The eight stream cells are always water: (72, 114) is row 5.
        for year in [800, 1150, 1350] {
            assert!(at(year)[5 * 80 + 72], "{year}");
        }
        // Type-3 points hold water only within their years (inclusive).
        let p = v.water_points().iter().find(|p| p.kind == 3).unwrap();
        let cell = (p.y * WIDTH + p.x) as usize;
        assert!(at(p.start as u32)[cell] && at(p.end as u32)[cell]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_historical_curve_is_the_bundled_one() {
        let v = valley();
        let mut years = 0;
        for line in HISTORICAL.lines() {
            let mut parts = line.split_whitespace().map(|t| t.parse::<u32>().unwrap());
            let (year, households) = (parts.next().unwrap(), parts.next().unwrap());
            assert_eq!(v.historical(year), households, "year {year}");
            years += 1;
        }
        assert_eq!(years, 551, "800 to 1350");
        // The extraction's checks (§5.6, D-7).
        let h = |y: u32| v.historical(y);
        assert_eq!(
            (h(800), h(840), h(850), h(1000), h(1050)),
            (14, 7, 28, 87, 134)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            (h(1108), h(1150), h(1269), h(1280), h(1290)),
            (167, 116, 216, 159, 95)
        );
        assert_eq!((800..=1350).map(h).max(), Some(216));
        assert!((1300..=1350).all(|y| h(y) == 0) && h(1299) > 0);
        assert_eq!((800..=1350).map(h).sum::<u32>(), 52_108);
        assert_eq!(v.historical(100), 0, "outside the data");
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_site_rises_to_its_median_and_falls_to_its_end() {
        // start 1000, end 1100, median 1050, 10 households at the median.
        let n = |y| site_households(1000, 1100, 1050, 10, y);
        assert_eq!(
            (n(999), n(1000), n(1001), n(1050), n(1051), n(1099), n(1100)),
            (0, 1, 1, 10, 10, 1, 0)
        );
        assert_eq!(n(1025), 5);
        // Median at the start: 0 up to it, then falling.
        assert_eq!(site_households(1000, 1100, 1000, 10, 1000), 0);
        assert_eq!(site_households(1000, 1100, 1000, 10, 1001), 10);
    }
}
```

In `crates/sugarscape-core/src/anasazi/valley.rs`, between the module doc and `#[cfg(test)]`, add:
```rust
use std::sync::OnceLock;

use serde::Serialize;

/// The grid: 80 columns west to east, 120 rows (JASSS ¶2.5).
pub const WIDTH: u32 = 80;
pub const HEIGHT: u32 = 120;
pub const CELLS: usize = (WIDTH * HEIGHT) as usize;
/// The years every data file covers (`environment.txt` starts in 382,
/// `adjustedPDSI.txt` in 200; both end in 1499; D-6).
pub const FIRST_YEAR: u32 = 382;
pub const LAST_YEAR: u32 = 1499;
const PDSI_FIRST_YEAR: u32 = 200;
const PDSI_YEARS: usize = 1300;
const ENV_RECORD: usize = 15;
const WATER_RECORD: usize = 6;
const SITE_RECORD: usize = 12;

const MAP: &str = include_str!("../../../../data/anasazi/Map.txt");
const PDSI: &str = include_str!("../../../../data/anasazi/adjustedPDSI.txt");
const ENVIRONMENT: &str = include_str!("../../../../data/anasazi/environment.txt");
const WATER: &str = include_str!("../../../../data/anasazi/water.txt");
const SETTLEMENTS: &str = include_str!("../../../../data/anasazi/settlements.txt");

/// A cell's land-cover zone (§5.2; ODD p.1).
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize)]
#[serde(rename_all = "snake_case")]
pub enum Zone {
    General,
    North,
    NorthDunes,
    Mid,
    MidDunes,
    /// Uplands Nonarable ("Natural").
    Natural,
    /// Arable Uplands.
    Uplands,
    Kinbiko,
    /// Outside the valley.
    Empty,
}

/// A zone's column of JASSS Table 3 (§3.2).
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum YieldColumn {
    /// North Valley, Mid Valley and Kinbiko Canyon.
    NorthMidKinbiko,
    General,
    Uplands,
    Dunes,
}

/// The four adjusted-PDSI series, in file block order (§5.3).
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum PdsiSeries {
    General = 0,
    North = 1,
    Mid = 2,
    Natural = 3,
}

impl Zone {
    pub const ALL: [Zone; 9] = [
        Zone::General,
        Zone::North,
        Zone::NorthDunes,
        Zone::Mid,
        Zone::MidDunes,
        Zone::Natural,
        Zone::Uplands,
        Zone::Kinbiko,
        Zone::Empty,
    ];

    /// The zone of a `Map.txt` code.
    pub fn from_code(code: u32) -> Option<Zone> {
        Some(match code {
            0 => Zone::General,
            10 => Zone::North,
            15 => Zone::NorthDunes,
            20 => Zone::Mid,
            25 => Zone::MidDunes,
            30 => Zone::Natural,
            40 => Zone::Uplands,
            50 => Zone::Kinbiko,
            60 => Zone::Empty,
            _ => return None,
        })
    }

    /// The ODD's name for the zone.
    pub fn name(self) -> &'static str {
        match self {
            Zone::General => "General Valley Floor",
            Zone::North => "North Valley Floor",
            Zone::NorthDunes => "North Valley Dunes",
            Zone::Mid => "Midvalley Floor",
            Zone::MidDunes => "Mid Valley Dunes",
            Zone::Natural => "Uplands Nonarable",
            Zone::Uplands => "Arable Uplands",
            Zone::Kinbiko => "Kinbiko Canyon",
            Zone::Empty => "Outside the valley",
        }
    }

    /// Its yield column; `None` for the zones that yield nothing
    /// (Nonarable Uplands and outside the valley).
    pub fn column(self) -> Option<YieldColumn> {
        Some(match self {
            Zone::North | Zone::Mid | Zone::Kinbiko => YieldColumn::NorthMidKinbiko,
            Zone::General => YieldColumn::General,
            Zone::Uplands => YieldColumn::Uplands,
            Zone::NorthDunes | Zone::MidDunes => YieldColumn::Dunes,
            Zone::Natural | Zone::Empty => return None,
        })
    }

    /// The PDSI series its yield follows (A-6, adopted from the replication):
    /// Kinbiko shares North's; the Dunes have none (their PDSI is 0, so they
    /// always yield the (−1, 1) class: JASSS ¶4.3's 855 kg); Arable Uplands
    /// follow the Natural series, or none when `uplands_single_class` (the
    /// replication's name mismatch) is on. `None` means a PDSI of 0.
    pub fn pdsi_series(self, uplands_single_class: bool) -> Option<PdsiSeries> {
        match self {
            Zone::General => Some(PdsiSeries::General),
            Zone::North | Zone::Kinbiko => Some(PdsiSeries::North),
            Zone::Mid => Some(PdsiSeries::Mid),
            Zone::Natural => Some(PdsiSeries::Natural),
            Zone::Uplands if !uplands_single_class => Some(PdsiSeries::Natural),
            _ => None,
        }
    }

    /// Its group in `environment.txt` (§5.4: General, North, Mid, Natural —
    /// also used for Arable Uplands — and Kinbiko); `None` (hydro 0) for the
    /// Dunes and outside the valley.
    fn hydro_group(self) -> Option<usize> {
        match self {
            Zone::General => Some(0),
            Zone::North => Some(1),
            Zone::Mid => Some(2),
            Zone::Natural | Zone::Uplands => Some(3),
            Zone::Kinbiko => Some(4),
            _ => None,
        }
    }
}

/// The PDSI class of JASSS ¶2.11 / ODD p.4, 0 (driest) to 4: (−∞, −3],
/// (−3, −1], (−1, 1), [1, 3), [3, ∞).
pub fn pdsi_class(pdsi: f64) -> usize {
    if pdsi <= -3.0 {
        0
    } else if pdsi <= -1.0 {
        1
    } else if pdsi < 1.0 {
        2
    } else if pdsi < 3.0 {
        3
    } else {
        4
    }
}

/// Yield in kg per cell per year by column and PDSI class (JASSS Table 3).
pub fn column_yield(column: YieldColumn, class: usize) -> f64 {
    const NORTH_MID_KINBIKO: [f64; 5] = [617.0, 719.0, 821.0, 988.0, 1153.0];
    const GENERAL: [f64; 5] = [514.0, 599.0, 684.0, 824.0, 961.0];
    const UPLANDS: [f64; 5] = [411.0, 479.0, 547.0, 659.0, 769.0];
    const DUNES: [f64; 5] = [642.0, 749.0, 855.0, 1030.0, 1201.0];
    let row = match column {
        YieldColumn::NorthMidKinbiko => &NORTH_MID_KINBIKO,
        YieldColumn::General => &GENERAL,
        YieldColumn::Uplands => &UPLANDS,
        YieldColumn::Dunes => &DUNES,
    };
    row[class]
}

/// A water point of `water.txt` (§5.5), on the grid.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub struct WaterPoint {
    pub x: u32,
    pub y: u32,
    /// 1–4; only 2 (permanent) and 3 (in `start..=end`) are ever water (A-5).
    pub kind: u32,
    pub start: i64,
    pub end: i64,
}

/// Water periods not in any data file (A-5, adopted from the replication),
/// as half-open year ranges: every General, North, Mid and Kinbiko cell is
/// water in the alluvium periods, every Kinbiko cell in the stream periods.
const ALLUVIUM: [(u32, u32); 4] = [(420, 560), (630, 680), (980, 1120), (1180, 1230)];
const STREAM: [(u32, u32); 3] = [(280, 360), (800, 930), (1300, 1450)];
/// Eight cells that are always water (A-5), in the data's (x, northward y):
/// a stream line into the North Valley.
const STREAM_CELLS: [(u32, u32); 8] = [
    (72, 114),
    (70, 113),
    (69, 112),
    (68, 111),
    (67, 110),
    (66, 109),
    (65, 108),
    (65, 107),
];

/// The valley's data, parsed once (`valley()`).
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct Valley {
    zones: Vec<Zone>,
    /// The four series, each indexed by `year - 200`.
    pdsi: [Vec<f64>; 4],
    /// Per year from 382, the five groups' hydro values (field 1 of each).
    hydro: Vec<[f64; 5]>,
    /// Every on-grid water point, of every type.
    water: Vec<WaterPoint>,
    /// Historical households per year from `FIRST_YEAR` to `LAST_YEAR`.
    historical: Vec<u32>,
}

fn tokens(name: &str, text: &str, record: usize, expected: usize) -> Result<Vec<f64>, String> {
    let values = text
        .split_whitespace()
        .map(|t| t.parse::<f64>().map_err(|e| format!("{name}: {t:?}: {e}")))
        .collect::<Result<Vec<f64>, String>>()?;
    if values.len() != expected || values.len() % record != 0 {
        return Err(format!(
            "{name}: {} numbers, expected {expected}",
            values.len()
        ));
    }
    Ok(values)
}

/// The cell of a point `n` meters north and `e` meters east in the data's
/// coordinates (§5.5's water conversion, A-8: 93.5 m per cell), or `None`
/// off the grid.
fn water_cell(n: f64, e: f64) -> Option<(u32, u32)> {
    let x = 25.0 + ((e - 2392.0) / 93.5).trunc();
    let north = 45.0 + (37.6 + (n - 7954.0) / 93.5).trunc();
    let on = |v: f64, n: u32| v >= 0.0 && v < f64::from(n);
    (on(x, WIDTH) && on(north, HEIGHT)).then(|| (x as u32, HEIGHT - 1 - north as u32))
}

/// `ceil(p / q)` for `p ≥ 0`, `q > 0`.
fn ceil_div(p: i64, q: i64) -> i64 {
    (p + q - 1) / q
}

/// One site's households in `year` (§5.6): a triangle rising from `start`
/// to the median and falling to `end`, rounded up and at least 1 while
/// occupied; a site whose median is its start counts 0 up to the median.
fn site_households(start: i64, end: i64, median: i64, baseline: i64, year: i64) -> i64 {
    if year < start || year >= end {
        return 0;
    }
    if year > median {
        return ceil_div(baseline * (end - year), end - median).max(1);
    }
    if median == start {
        return 0;
    }
    ceil_div(baseline * (year - start), median - start).max(1)
}

impl Valley {
    /// Parses the five files (their text), checking each one's size.
    pub fn parse(
        map: &str,
        pdsi: &str,
        environment: &str,
        water: &str,
        settlements: &str,
    ) -> Result<Valley, String> {
        let codes = tokens("Map.txt", map, 1, CELLS)?;
        let mut zones = vec![Zone::Empty; CELLS];
        for (k, &code) in codes.iter().enumerate() {
            let zone = Zone::from_code(code as u32)
                .filter(|_| code.fract() == 0.0)
                .ok_or_else(|| format!("Map.txt: unknown zone code {code}"))?;
            // Column-major, each column north to south, columns west to east.
            let (x, y) = (k / HEIGHT as usize, k % HEIGHT as usize);
            zones[y * WIDTH as usize + x] = zone;
        }
        let series = tokens("adjustedPDSI.txt", pdsi, PDSI_YEARS, 4 * PDSI_YEARS)?;
        let pdsi = [0, 1, 2, 3].map(|b| series[b * PDSI_YEARS..(b + 1) * PDSI_YEARS].to_vec());
        let years = (LAST_YEAR - FIRST_YEAR + 1) as usize;
        let env = tokens(
            "environment.txt",
            environment,
            ENV_RECORD,
            years * ENV_RECORD,
        )?;
        let hydro = env
            .chunks_exact(ENV_RECORD)
            .map(|r| [r[1], r[4], r[7], r[10], r[13]])
            .collect();
        let points = tokens("water.txt", water, WATER_RECORD, 108 * WATER_RECORD)?;
        let water = points
            .chunks_exact(WATER_RECORD)
            .filter_map(|r| {
                // id, meters north, meters east, type, start, end
                water_cell(r[1], r[2]).map(|(x, y)| WaterPoint {
                    x,
                    y,
                    kind: r[3] as u32,
                    start: r[4] as i64,
                    end: r[5] as i64,
                })
            })
            .collect();
        let sites = tokens(
            "settlements.txt",
            settlements,
            SITE_RECORD,
            488 * SITE_RECORD,
        )?;
        let historical = (FIRST_YEAR..=LAST_YEAR)
            .map(|year| {
                sites
                    .chunks_exact(SITE_RECORD)
                    // Field 6 is the type: only habitations (1) count.
                    .filter(|r| r[6] == 1.0)
                    .map(|r| {
                        // start, end, median (years BP) and baseline households.
                        let (start, end) = (r[3] as i64, r[4] as i64);
                        let median = 1950 - r[5] as i64;
                        site_households(start, end, median, r[11] as i64, i64::from(year))
                    })
                    .sum::<i64>() as u32
            })
            .collect();
        Ok(Valley {
            zones,
            pdsi,
            hydro,
            water,
            historical,
        })
    }

    pub fn zone(&self, cell: usize) -> Zone {
        self.zones[cell]
    }

    /// Series `s`'s adjusted PDSI in `year` (200–1499).
    pub fn pdsi(&self, s: PdsiSeries, year: u32) -> f64 {
        self.pdsi[s as usize][(year - PDSI_FIRST_YEAR) as usize]
    }

    /// `zone`'s PDSI in `year` (0 for a zone without a series; A-6).
    pub fn zone_pdsi(&self, zone: Zone, year: u32, uplands_single_class: bool) -> f64 {
        zone.pdsi_series(uplands_single_class)
            .map_or(0.0, |s| self.pdsi(s, year))
    }

    /// `zone`'s hydrologic value in `year` (§5.4; 0 for the Dunes and
    /// outside the valley).
    pub fn hydro(&self, zone: Zone, year: u32) -> f64 {
        zone.hydro_group()
            .map_or(0.0, |g| self.hydro[(year - FIRST_YEAR) as usize][g])
    }

    /// Every on-grid point of `water.txt`.
    pub fn water_points(&self) -> &[WaterPoint] {
        &self.water
    }

    /// Which cells are water sources in `year` (A-5): type-2 points always,
    /// type-3 points from their start to their end year inclusive, every
    /// General, North, Mid and Kinbiko cell in an alluvium period, every
    /// Kinbiko cell in a stream period, and the eight stream cells.
    pub fn water_sources(&self, year: u32) -> Vec<bool> {
        let within = |periods: &[(u32, u32)]| periods.iter().any(|&(a, b)| (a..b).contains(&year));
        let (alluvium, stream) = (within(&ALLUVIUM), within(&STREAM));
        let mut out: Vec<bool> = self
            .zones
            .iter()
            .map(|z| match z {
                Zone::General | Zone::North | Zone::Mid => alluvium,
                Zone::Kinbiko => alluvium || stream,
                _ => false,
            })
            .collect();
        let y = i64::from(year);
        for p in &self.water {
            if p.kind == 2 || (p.kind == 3 && (p.start..=p.end).contains(&y)) {
                out[(p.y * WIDTH + p.x) as usize] = true;
            }
        }
        for (x, north) in STREAM_CELLS {
            out[((HEIGHT - 1 - north) * WIDTH + x) as usize] = true;
        }
        out
    }

    /// The archaeological estimate of households in `year` (§5.6), 0
    /// outside `FIRST_YEAR..=LAST_YEAR`.
    pub fn historical(&self, year: u32) -> u32 {
        year.checked_sub(FIRST_YEAR)
            .and_then(|k| self.historical.get(k as usize))
            .copied()
            .unwrap_or(0)
    }
}

/// The bundled valley, parsed on first use.
pub fn valley() -> &'static Valley {
    static VALLEY: OnceLock<Valley> = OnceLock::new();
    VALLEY.get_or_init(|| {
        Valley::parse(MAP, PDSI, ENVIRONMENT, WATER, SETTLEMENTS).expect("the bundled data parse")
    })
}
```

Notes for the reviewer: the water rules (`ALLUVIUM`, `STREAM`, `STREAM_CELLS`, types 2 and 3 only) are the replication's, adopted in every mode (A-5, Decision 6); `Zone::pdsi_series` is A-6 (Kinbiko on North's series, the Dunes on none, Arable Uplands on Natural's unless `uplands_single_class`); `hydro` reads field 1 of each environment group (§5.4). Everything is read from the extraction, not the NetLogo code.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

Create `crates/sugarscape-core/src/anasazi/household.rs` with its module doc and tests:
```rust
//! A household (§1.1: five persons, one farm plot, one residence) and the
//! documented rules that concern only its own corn: storage aging and
//! consumption (ODD p.4–5, §3.5), the expected harvest (§3.7) and the
//! fission endowment (§3.10).

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn storage_ages_and_the_oldest_leftovers_spoil() {
        let mut corn = vec![300.0, 200.0, 100.0];
        store_harvest(&mut corn, 900.0);
        assert_eq!(
            corn,
            [900.0, 300.0, 200.0],
            "the 100 kg left in S-2 spoiled"
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn households_eat_the_oldest_corn_first() {
        let mut corn = vec![900.0, 300.0, 200.0];
        assert_eq!(consume(&mut corn, 800.0), 0.0);
        assert_eq!(corn, [600.0, 0.0, 0.0]);
        let mut short = vec![100.0, 50.0, 50.0];
        assert_eq!(
            consume(&mut short, 800.0),
            600.0,
            "NNR left: the household is removed"
        );
        assert_eq!(short, [0.0, 0.0, 0.0]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_negative_harvest_is_kept_and_adds_to_the_unmet_need() {
        // A-14 (adopted): harvests are not clamped at 0, and the ODD's
        // algorithm turns a negative S0 into unmet need even when older
        // corn covered the meal.
        let mut corn = vec![0.0, 900.0, 0.0];
        store_harvest(&mut corn, -50.0);
        assert_eq!(corn, [-50.0, 0.0, 900.0]);
        assert_eq!(consume(&mut corn, 800.0), 50.0);
        assert_eq!(corn, [0.0, 0.0, 100.0]);
        assert_eq!(expected_harvest(&[-50.0, 0.0, 100.0], -50.0), -100.0);
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_expected_harvest_counts_the_current_harvest_twice() {
        // §3.7: S0 already holds what is left of this year's harvest.
        let corn = vec![100.0, 50.0, 400.0];
        assert_eq!(
            expected_harvest(&corn, 900.0),
            1050.0,
            "S-2 spoils and is excluded"
        );
        assert_eq!(expected_harvest(&[70.0], 900.0), 900.0, "no storage");
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_documented_endowment_conserves_corn() {
        let mut parent = vec![900.0, 300.0, 30.0];
        let child = split_endowment(&mut parent, 0.25);
        assert_eq!(child, [225.0, 75.0, 7.5]);
        assert_eq!(parent, [675.0, 225.0, 22.5]);
        let total: f64 = parent.iter().chain(&child).sum();
        assert!((total - 1230.0).abs() < 1e-9);
    }
}
```

Create `crates/sugarscape-core/src/anasazi/random.rs` with its module doc and tests:
```rust
//! Normal draws that are bit-for-bit the same on every platform. `f64::ln`
//! calls the platform's math library, which may differ in the last bit
//! between a native build and wasm32; the harvest noise feeds every
//! fingerprint, so the logarithm here uses only IEEE-exact arithmetic.

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use crate::rng;

    #[test]
    fn ln_matches_the_library_to_a_few_ulps() {
        let mut r = rng::seeded(7);
        let mut xs: Vec<f64> = (0..20_000)
            .map(|_| r.gen::<f64>())
            .filter(|&x| x > 0.0)
            .collect();
        xs.extend([
            1.0,
            0.5,
            2.0,
            1e-300,
            1e300,
            std::f64::consts::E,
            1.0 + 1e-12,
            0.999_999,
        ]);
        for x in xs {
            let (ours, std) = (ln(x), x.ln());
            assert!(
                (ours - std).abs() <= 4.0 * f64::EPSILON * std.abs().max(1e-300),
                "{x}: {ours} vs {std}"
            );
        }
        assert_eq!(ln(1.0), 0.0);
    }

    #[test]
    fn normal_draws_have_mean_0_and_sd_1() {
        let mut r = rng::seeded(11);
        let n = 200_000;
        let draws: Vec<f64> = (0..n).map(|_| normal(&mut r)).collect();
        let mean = draws.iter().sum::<f64>() / n as f64;
        let sd = (draws.iter().map(|d| (d - mean).powi(2)).sum::<f64>() / n as f64).sqrt();
        assert!(mean.abs() < 0.01, "mean {mean}");
        assert!((sd - 1.0).abs() < 0.01, "sd {sd}");
        let below = draws.iter().filter(|&&d| d < -1.0).count() as f64 / n as f64;
        assert!((below - 0.1587).abs() < 0.005, "P(z < −1) = {below}");
    }

    #[test]
    fn draws_are_a_function_of_the_seed() {
        let a: Vec<f64> = {
            let mut r = rng::seeded(3);
            (0..5).map(|_| normal(&mut r)).collect()
        };
        let mut r = rng::seeded(3);
        assert_eq!(a, (0..5).map(|_| normal(&mut r)).collect::<Vec<_>>());
    }
}
```

Create `crates/sugarscape-core/src/anasazi/config.rs` with its module doc and tests:
```rust
//! The anasazi model's parameters (§2) and the replication's departures
//! from the written description as named switches (§7).

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn presets_values_are_the_papers() {
        let t2 = AnasaziConfig::table_2(Quirks::ALL);
        assert_eq!(
            (t2.need, t2.death_age, t2.fertility_start, t2.fertility_end),
            (800.0, 30, 16, 30)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            (
                t2.fission_probability,
                t2.child_endowment,
                t2.harvest_adjustment
            ),
            (0.125, 0.33, 1.0)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            (t2.spatial_sd, t2.annual_sd, t2.storage_years),
            (0.1, 0.1, 2)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            (t2.start_year, t2.end_year, t2.initial_households),
            (800, 1350, 14)
        );
        let cal = AnasaziConfig::default();
        assert_eq!((cal.death_age, cal.fertility_end), (38, 34));
        assert_eq!(
            (
                cal.fission_probability,
                cal.harvest_adjustment,
                cal.annual_sd
            ),
            (0.155, 0.56, 0.4)
        );
        assert_eq!(cal.quirks, Quirks::ALL);
        assert_eq!(AnasaziConfig::calibrated(Quirks::NONE).quirks, Quirks::NONE);
    }

    #[test]
    fn quirks_default_to_on_and_are_named_in_order() {
        let q: Quirks = serde_json::from_str(r#"{"wrap_edges": false}"#).unwrap();
        assert!(!q.wrap_edges && q.occupancy_leak);
        let json = serde_json::to_value(Quirks::NONE).unwrap();
        let keys: Vec<&str> = json
            .as_object()
            .unwrap()
            .keys()
            .map(|k| k.as_str())
            .collect();
        let mut names = Quirks::NAMES.to_vec();
        names.sort_unstable();
        let mut sorted = keys.clone();
        sorted.sort_unstable();
        assert_eq!(sorted, names);
        assert!(serde_json::from_str::<Quirks>(r#"{"nope": true}"#).is_err());
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_soil_sd_is_the_spatial_one_unless_one_sd_is_used() {
        let mut c = AnasaziConfig::table_2(Quirks::NONE);
        c.spatial_sd = 0.2;
        c.annual_sd = 0.3;
        assert_eq!(c.soil_sd(), 0.2);
        c.quirks.single_harvest_variance = true;
        assert_eq!(c.soil_sd(), 0.3);
    }

    #[test]
    fn validation_names_fields() {
        let bad = AnasaziConfig {
            start_year: 300,
            end_year: 1600,
            initial_households: 5000,
            initial_corn: CornRange {
                min: 10.0,
                max: 5.0,
            },
            need: 0.0,
            fertility_start: 40,
            fertility_end: 30,
            death_age: 0,
            fission_probability: 1.5,
            child_endowment: 1.0,
            storage_years: 11,
            harvest_adjustment: -1.0,
            spatial_sd: f64::NAN,
            annual_sd: 11.0,
            quirks: Quirks::NONE,
        };
        let fields: Vec<String> = bad
            .validate()
            .unwrap_err()
            .into_iter()
            .map(|e| e.field)
            .collect();
        assert_eq!(
            fields,
            [
                "start_year",
                "end_year",
                "initial_households",
                "initial_corn",
                "need",
                "fertility_end",
                "death_age",
                "fission_probability",
                "child_endowment",
                "storage_years",
                "harvest_adjustment",
                "spatial_sd",
                "annual_sd"
            ]
        );
        assert!(AnasaziConfig::default().validate().is_ok());
        let e = AnasaziConfig {
            end_year: 800,
            ..AnasaziConfig::default()
        };
        assert_eq!(e.validate().unwrap_err()[0].field, "end_year");
    }

    #[test]
    fn only_the_harvest_changes_live() {
        let c = AnasaziConfig::default();
        let live = AnasaziConfig {
            harvest_adjustment: 0.6,
            annual_sd: 0.2,
            ..c.clone()
        };
        assert!(c.changes(&live).is_empty());
        let mut reset = live.clone();
        reset.death_age = 30;
        reset.quirks.wrap_edges = false;
        let fields: Vec<String> = c.changes(&reset).into_iter().map(|e| e.field).collect();
        assert_eq!(fields, ["death_age", "quirks"]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn every_quirk_has_a_path_label_and_help() {
        let quirks: Vec<Param> = schema()
            .into_iter()
            .filter(|p| p.group == "Replication quirks")
            .collect();
        let paths: Vec<&str> = quirks.iter().map(|p| p.path).collect();
        let expected: Vec<String> = Quirks::NAMES
            .iter()
            .map(|n| format!("quirks.{n}"))
            .collect();
        assert_eq!(paths, expected);
        for p in &quirks {
            let help = p.help.unwrap();
            assert!(
                help.ends_with(").") && help.contains("(A-"),
                "{}: {help}",
                p.path
            );
        }
    }
}
```
